\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[2\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.87111pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.26662pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\right]\right)\mathrm{d}\xi

(6.21)

for all positive constants
T
and
R
.
Especially,

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right),

(6.22)

where

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

\displaystyle\iff H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,T]\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)\quad\forall T\in(0,\infty).

If additionally
\exp\left(\left|\int_{0}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)
is locally bounded, i.e.

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in[0,T],\xi\in B_{R}}\left[\exp\left(\left|\int_{0}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\right]<\infty\quad\forall T,R\in(0,\infty),

(6.23)

then

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),

(6.24)

where

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

\displaystyle\iff H\in\mathbb{L}_{1}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)\quad\forall T\in(0,\infty).

Proof.

By Corollary
6.5
,
H
is
\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable and satisfies (
6.12
) and (
6.15
).
Thus it suffices to show (
6.19
), (
6.20
), (
6.21
), and (
6.24
).
Due to the joint measurability, we can apply the Fubini theorem with (
6.12
).
Indeed, for all
T\in(0,\infty)
and
R\in(0,\infty)
, applying the Fubini theorem, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||H(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathbb{E}\left[|H(t,\xi)|\right]\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[2\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right]<\infty,

(6.25)

where the last finiteness comes from (
6.18
).
Similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}\left|H(t,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq T\cdot C_{BDG}\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

Therefore, we have (
6.19
), (
6.20
), and (
6.22
).

Next we show (
6.21
).
Let
R,T\in(0,\infty)
.
Taking the integration
\int_{B_{R}}\cdot\mathrm{d}\xi
to the both sides of (
6.15
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|H(t,\xi)|\right]\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[2\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\right]\right)\mathrm{d}\xi,

(6.26)

which directly implies (
6.21
).

At last, we prove (
6.24
).
Since the
\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurability had already been obtained, and
the additional condition in (
6.23
) and the Fubini theorem with (
6.17
) and (
6.21
) imply

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|H(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]<\infty,

it is sufficient to to show that the continuity of the paths.
It is straightforward that for almost every
\omega\in\Omega
and
\xi\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
,
the both mappings

t\in[0,\infty)\mapsto H_{1}(t,\xi)\quad\text{and}\quad t\in[0,\infty)\mapsto H_{2}(t,\xi)

are continuous with respect to
t
due to the properties of the Lebesgue integral and the Itô stochastic integral, respectively.
Therefore, the product
H
of
H_{1}
and
H_{2}
is also continuous with respect to
t
for almost every
(\omega,\xi)\in\Omega\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
.
The corollary is proved.
∎

Remark 6.8
.

We reexamine the joint measurability issues to assess the validity of our stronger assumptions.
The stronger conditions (
6.17
) and (
6.23
) obviously imply the previous weaker condition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{T}\Re[2\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]<\infty\quad\forall T\in[0,\infty).

(6.27)

Additionally, recalling

\displaystyle H(t,\xi)=\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)G^{k}(s,\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}

and applying (
6.12
), for almost every
\xi
and
t
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|H(t,\xi)|\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[2\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\right].

(6.28)

However, (
6.27
) alone was not sufficient to ensure that
H(t,\xi)
is
\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable as mentioned in Remark
6.4
.
Therefore (
6.28
) does not generally imply

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|H(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[2\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

since the Fubini theorem is not applicable in this case.
For this reason, we imposed stronger conditions (
6.17
) and (
6.23
) to guarantee that
H(t,\xi)
is
\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable (indeed
\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable).
The joint measurability was importantly used in (
6.25
) to apply the Fubini theorem.

Additionally, (
6.23
) is not necessary if we merely have an interest in the joint measurability as shown in the previous corollaries.
(
6.6
) and (
6.17
) are enough to show the joint measurability.
Furthermore, (
6.6
) and (
6.17
) are also sufficient to ensure the path continuity so that for almost every
\xi
and
\omega
,
t\mapsto H(t,\xi)
is continuous.
However, continuity of its integration such as
t\mapsto\int_{B_{R}}H(t,\xi)\mathrm{d}\xi
and
t\mapsto\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}H(t,\xi)\mathrm{d}\xi\right]
is not guaranteed without (
6.23
).
This is another important property for
H
to be a
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-valued continuous processes as in Definition
2.25
.
Therefore both (
6.23
) and (
6.6
) are considered to preserve both the joint measurability and the
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-valued continuity of paths simultaneously.

Remark 6.9
.

Suppose that all assumptions in Corollary
6.7
are satisfied.
Then for any bounded stopping time
\tilde{\tau}
, it obviously holds that

H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tilde{\tau}\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

However, for a finite stopping time
\tilde{\tau}
, it is generally not true that

H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tilde{\tau}\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

This is because the BDG inequality does not ensure that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[1_{t\leq\tilde{\tau}}|H_{2}(t,\xi)|\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[1_{t\leq\tilde{\tau}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left|\exp\left(-\int_{0}^{s}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)G(s,\xi)\right|_{l_{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}\right]

due to the additional random term
1_{t\leq\tilde{\tau}}
.
Specifically,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[1_{t\leq\tilde{\tau}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left|\exp\left(-\int_{0}^{s}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)G(s,\xi)\right|_{l_{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau}\left|\exp\left(-\int_{0}^{s}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)G(s,\xi)\right|_{l_{2}}ds\right)^{1/2}\right]

and the equality does not generally hold.

Now we compile all the corollaries to prepare the stability results for stochastic inhomogeneous data whose spatial Fourier transform has a finite expectation.

Corollary 6.10
.

Let
G\in\mathbb{L}^{\omega,x,t}_{1,1,2,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d});l_{2}\right)
.
Suppose that
\tilde{\psi}
satisfies (
6.4
) and (
6.5
).
Denote

\displaystyle H(t,\xi)=\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.87111pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.26662pt$]$}}(s)G^{k}(s,\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}.

Then
H
belongs to the intersection of the classes

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

Moreover, for all positive constants
T
and
R
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|H(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq T\cdot C_{BDG}\esssup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left[\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.87111pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.26662pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right],

(6.29)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|H(t,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]\leq\esssup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\exp\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right],

(6.30)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||H(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\esssup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\mathrm{d}t\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

(6.31)

Proof.

Since

G\in\mathbb{L}^{\omega,x,t}_{1,1,2,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d});l_{2}\right)

and
\tilde{\psi}
satisfies (
6.4
) and (
6.5
), for all
R,T\in(0,\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\exp\left(-\int_{0}^{s}\Re[2\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq\esssup_{0\leq s\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\exp\left(\left|\int_{0}^{s}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]<\infty,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[2\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\mathrm{d}t\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq\esssup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\mathrm{d}t\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle<\infty,

\displaystyle\esssup_{t\in[0,T],\xi\in B_{R}}\left[\exp\left(\left|\int_{0}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\right]<\infty,

and

\displaystyle\esssup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\right)\leq\esssup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\exp\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\mathrm{d}t\right)<\infty.

Thus by Corollary
6.7
, we have

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

Moreover, we apply (
6.20
), (
6.19
), and (
6.21
) from Corollary
6.7
to obtain (
6.29
), (
6.30
), and (
6.31
).
Indeed, for all
T,R\in(0,\infty)
, applying (
6.20
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|H(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[2\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq T\cdot C_{BDG}\esssup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left[\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

Additionally, by (
6.19
) and (
6.21
) with the Fubini theorem,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||H(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[2\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\esssup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\mathrm{d}t\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left|H(t,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[2\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\right]\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\esssup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\exp\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|G(s,\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

for all
R,T\in(0,\infty)
. The corollary is proved.
∎

Remark 6.11
.

It may seem that (
6.30
) is superior to (
6.29
).
However, (
6.30
) does not imply (
6.29
) since

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}\left[\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\leq\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}\left[\exp\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\right].

Remark 6.12
.

If
\tilde{\psi}
satisfies (
6.4
), then the two conditions (
6.17
) and (
6.27
) become equivalent.

Remark 6.13
.

The condition in (
6.5
) is sufficiently given to ensure that (
6.6
) holds, which importantly used to show the joint measurability and continuity of paths.
If one merely wants to obtain (
6.31
), then (
6.5
) can be slightly relaxed to

\displaystyle\esssup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\mathrm{d}t\right)<\infty.

(6.32)

Additionally, (
6.5
) can be perfectly replaced with (
6.6
) and (
6.32
) if one also wishes to maintain the continuity of paths.
This generalization could be meaningful due to logarithmic operators as mentioned in Remark
3.24
.

7.
Uniqueness of a solution

In this section, we examine the uniqueness of a Fourier-space weak solution to (
4.1
). We demonstrate that the uniqueness holds in a broader class than the one in which a solution exists as stated in Theorem
4.3
. We assume throughout this section that a Fourier-space weak solution exists. However, understanding the strength of this assumption is challenging.
At a minimum, it ensures that the function
\psi(t,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)
is locally integrable, based on the definition of the solution if
u
is a Fourier-space weak solution.

Surprisingly, this existence assumption alone is sufficient to guarantee uniqueness without requiring additional assumptions on the symbol
\psi
.
In other words, we only assume that our symbol
\psi
is a complex-valued

\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable function defined on
\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
.
In particular, our symbol is random in this section.

Our approach is straightforward. First, we show that our solution has a strong form of representation in terms of data concerning their spatial frequencies, which is derived from the equation. Then, we apply the classical Grönwall inequality to prove the uniqueness. This is feasible because our class of test functions
\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
is sufficient to find a good approximation of the identity (Sobolev’s mollifier). Here is our representation lemma.

Lemma 7.1

(A representation of a solution from the equation)
.

Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)
,

\displaystyle f\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),~g\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}^{\omega,\xi,t}_{0,1,2,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d});l_{2}\right),

and
u
be a Fourier-space weak solution to (
4.1
).
Assume that

\displaystyle u\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\psi(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}\quad(a.e.)~(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}.

(7.1)

In particular, (
7.1
) shows that

\displaystyle u\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

due to the stochastic Fubini theorem and there exists a continuous (with respect to
t
) modification of
\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
.
Then by using the definition of a Fourier-space solution (Definition
4.1
), the definition of the Fourier transform, and assumptions on
u
,
u_{0}
,
f
, and
g
,
we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)],\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{F}[u_{0}],\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi),\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left(\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)],\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left(\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)],\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}\quad(a.e.)\quad(\omega,t)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$},

(7.2)

where
(\cdot,\cdot)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}
denotes the
L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-product, for instance,

\displaystyle\left(\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi),\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}=\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi.

Here the generalized Minkowski inequality

\displaystyle\left(\int_{0}^{T}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(t)\left(\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(\omega,t,\cdot)](\xi)\right|_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}\xi\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\leq\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(t)\left|\mathcal{F}[g(\omega,t,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi

is applied to show

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)],\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right\rangle=\left(\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)],\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\quad\forall k\in\mathbf{N}.

Additionally, by the (stochastic) Fubini theorem, (
7.2
) implies

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)],\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{F}[u_{0}],\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s,\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)]\mathrm{d}s,\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\int_{0}^{t}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)]\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s},\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\quad(a.e.)\quad(\omega,t)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}.

Moreover by using the separability of
\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)],\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{F}[u_{0}],\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s,\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)]\mathrm{d}s,\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\int_{0}^{t}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)]\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s},\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right)_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\quad(a.e.)\quad(\omega,t)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\quad\forall\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d}).

(7.3)

Next, we use a Sobolev mollifier with additional specific properties.
Let
\chi
be a function in
\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{R}^{d})

so that
\mathcal{F}[\chi]
is non-negative and symmetric,
i.e.

\mathcal{F}[\chi](\xi)=\mathcal{F}[\chi](-\xi)\geq 0
for all
\xi\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
.
Additionally, assume that
\mathcal{F}[\chi]
has a compact support and

\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\chi(x)\mathrm{d}x=(2\pi)^{d/2}.

For
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, denote

\displaystyle\chi^{\varepsilon}(x):=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{d}}\chi\left(\frac{x}{\varepsilon}\right).

Fix
x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
and put
\chi^{\varepsilon}(x-\cdot)
in (
7.3
) instead of
\varphi
.
Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}(x-\cdot)](\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}(x-\cdot)](\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}(x-\cdot)](\xi)}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}(x-\cdot)](\xi)}\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}(x-\cdot)](\xi)}\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}\mathrm{d}\xi

for almost every
(\omega,t)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}
.
Thus recalling properties of
\mathcal{F}[\chi]
and the Fourier transform, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}x\cdot\xi}\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}](\xi)\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}x\cdot\xi}\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}](\xi)\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}x\cdot\xi}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}](\xi)\mathrm{d}s\right)\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}x\cdot\xi}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}](\xi)\mathrm{d}s\right)\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}x\cdot\xi}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}\xi

(7.4)

for almost every
(\omega,t)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}
.
Note that both sides of (
7.4
) are continuous with respect to
x
, which implies that (
7.4
) holds for all
x
.
Therefore, due to the Fourier inversion theorem,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}](\xi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}](\xi)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}](\xi)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}

(7.5)

for almost every
(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
.
Observe that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\chi^{\varepsilon}](\xi)=\mathcal{F}[\chi](\varepsilon\xi)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\chi](0)=(2\pi)^{-d/2}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\chi(y)\mathrm{d}y=1.

Finally, taking
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
in (
7.5
), we have
(
7.1
). The lemma is proved.
∎

From the representation above, we obtain important two theorems.
The first one is
a prior estimate
and the second one is
uniqueness of a solution
.

We present a deterministic version of
a prior estimate
first.
Notably, it is feasible to determine the explicit constant in
a prior estimate
if there is no noise term.

Theorem 7.2

((Deterministic) A priori estimate)
.

Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)
,

f\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),

and
u
be a Fourier-space weak solution to (
3.13
).
Assume that for all
R\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{B_{R}}|\psi(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi+C_{1}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi

(7.6)

with probability one, where
C_{0}
and
C_{1}
are positive constants.
Then

u\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and for all
R\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau]}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{0})\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi+(1+C_{1})\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{0}\vee C_{1})\left(\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

(7.7)

with probability one.

Proof.

(
7.6
) obviously implies

\displaystyle u\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\psi(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

Thus by applying Lemma
7.1
, it suffices to show (
7.7
).
By (
7.1
), there exists a continuous modification of
\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)
so that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s\quad(a.e.)~(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}

and it implies

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|+\int_{0}^{t}|\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}s\quad(a.e.)~(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}.

(7.8)

Taking the integral and the essential supremum to both sides of (
7.8
), and using (
7.6
), we have

\displaystyle\left[\int_{B_{R}}\esssup_{t\in[0,\tau]}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]+\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]\Bigg)+\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{0})\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]+(1+C_{1})\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

Finally, using the continuity of the modification of
\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)
, we have (
7.7
).
The theorem is proved.
∎

Continuously, we present a stochastic version of
a prior estimate
.

Theorem 7.3

((Stochastic) A priori estimate)
.

Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)
,

f\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),

g\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}^{\omega,\xi,t}_{1,1,2,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d});l_{2}\right),

and
u
be a Fourier-space weak solution to (
4.1
).
Assume that for each
R,T\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\psi(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]+C_{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right],

(7.9)

where
C_{0}
,
C_{1}
, and
C_{2}
are positive constants.
Then

u\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and for all
R,T\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{0})\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]+(1+C_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(C_{BDG}+C_{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq(C_{BDG}+\max\{C_{0},C_{1},C_{2}\})\Bigg(\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]\Bigg).

(7.10)

Proof.

Due to Lemma
7.1
, it is sufficient to show (
7.10
).
By (
7.1
),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}\quad(a.e.)~(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}

and the right-hand side becomes a continuous (with respect to
t
) modification of
\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)]
.
For each
T\in(0,\infty)
, considering the modification, applying the above equality and the BDG inequality, taking the integration with respect to
\xi
on each
B_{R}
, and using the Fubini theorem with (
7.9
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}s\right]+C_{BDG}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq(1+C_{0})\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]+(1+C_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}s\right]

(7.11)

\displaystyle\quad+(C_{BDG}+C_{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

Therefore, (
3.8
) completes (
7.10
).
The theorem is proved.
∎

Remark 7.4
.

The last inequality in (
7.10
) is not utilized in the paper and does not seem to be optimal, since
it is believed that
C_{BDG}>1
.
Instead, the second inequality in (
7.10
) is used to estimate a solution.
In particular, (
7.10
) directly implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq T(1+C_{0})\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]+T(1+C_{1})\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+T(C_{BDG}+C_{2})\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

Remark 7.5
.

Recall that a solution
u
to (
4.1
) can be alternatively represented by using kernels related to

\int_{s}^{t}\psi(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r
, as shown in (
6.1
) if the symbol is non-random.
It is also feasible to estimate

u
based on this representation of
u
with the kernels, as demonstrated in Corollary
6.10
. This type of estimate is even simpler to obtain for deterministic terms.
An example appears in Lemma
8.1
.

On the other hand, note that (
7.1
) is used to obtain Theorem
7.3
.
As can be easily seen, (
7.1
) is rather straightforwardly linked with equation (
4.1
).
Additionally, they do not involve kernels directly, unlike (
6.1
).
Thus these-type estimates are referred to as “kernel-free estimates”.
Nowadays, there are many well-known kernel-free estimates to obtain
a priori estimate
of a solution.

Especially, there are numerous well-established kernel-free estimates that provide
a priori
estimates for solutions of PDEs (
cf.

[
17
,
19
,
20
,
34
]
). These estimates are particularly beneficial for complex equations where explicit kernel forms are unavailable.

Additionally, simple kernel-free estimates for SPDEs can be derived by applying Itô’s formula to compositions of smooth functions and solutions. However, recent kernel-free estimates developed for PDEs cannot be applied to SPDEs, as they often depend on local estimates that are not valid for SPDEs.

Overall, it remains uncertain whether ”kernel-based” or ”kernel-free” methods yield better estimates with respect to the size of their constants.
Specifically, we are unsure which constant derived from Corollary
6.10
or Theorem
7.3
is smaller at first glance.
To determine the most optimal constants, we compare all constants from our main theorems.
In particular, we utilize the straightforward relationship between constants provided in Remark
3.4
and (
3.14
) to simplify the constants in our estimates.
Generally, the constants from kernel-based estimates tend to be smaller.
However, this is not universally true for all estimates, as some conditions on
\psi
are refined due to joint measurability rather than the estimates themselves.
Additionally, if the symbol is random, a solution
u
is not expected to have a representation with the kernels, as emphasized several times.
In such cases, kernel-free estimates could be crucial for obtaining better estimates of the solution.

Now we show the uniqueness of the Fourier-space weak solution.
All data in the uniqueness theorem can be more easily generalized than those in the representation theorem due to the linearity of the equations.

Theorem 7.6

(Uniqueness of a Fourier-space weak solution)
.

Let
u_{0}\in\mathbb{L}_{0}\left(\Omega,\mathscr{F};\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)
,

f\in\mathbb{L}_{0,1,loc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}\left((0,\infty)\right);\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)\text{,~and}~g\in\mathbb{L}_{0,1,loc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$},\mathcal{P};\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d};l_{2})\right).

Then a Fourier-space weak solution to (
4.1
) is unique in the intersection of the spaces

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\psi(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,0,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right).

Proof.

Let
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
be Fourier-space weak solutions to (
4.1
) such that both
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
belong to

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\psi(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,0,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right).

Put

u=u_{1}-u_{2}.

Then by Lemma
7.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)=\int_{0}^{t}\psi(s,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s\quad(a.e.)~(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}.

Due to Grönwall’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)=0\quad(a.e.)~(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}.

(7.12)

Thus

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u_{1}(t,\cdot)](\xi)=\mathcal{F}[u_{2}(t,\cdot)](\xi)\quad(a.e.)~(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},

(7.13)

which obviously implies that
u_{1}=u_{2}
as an element in both spaces

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\psi(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,0,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right).

The theorem is proved.
∎

Remark 7.7
.

It may appear that (
7.12
) trivially implies (
7.13
).
However, if both solutions
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
are merely in

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\psi(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right),

then this implication does not generally hold.
It is because we do not know if both
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
have realizable spatial frequency functions on the whole set
\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
due to the possibility that
\psi(t,\xi)=0
for some points as partially noted in Remark
2.22
.
Thus the other class

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,0,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right).

is crucially used to ensure both
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
have realizable spatial frequency functions on the whole set.
Hence the implication is obtained from the fact that both
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
are elements in the above class.

Moreover, it is clear that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)\subset\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,0,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

as discussed in Remark
2.30
.
Therefore, the solution
u
in Lemma
7.1
is unique in the class, and the condition

\displaystyle u_{1},u_{2}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,0,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

in Theorem
7.6
can be omitted if the data
u_{0}
,
f
, and
g
are in smaller spaces as provided in Lemma
7.1
,
i.e.

\displaystyle u_{0}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),\quad f\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),

and

\displaystyle g\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}^{\omega,\xi,t}_{0,1,2,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d});l_{2}\right).

8.
Existence of a solution with a deterministic symbol

For functions to be Itô integrable, they must exhibit good measurability properties, such as predictability or progressive measurability. However, many integrands of the stochastic integral terms presented in Section
6
lose their predictability when the symbols are allowed to be random. In other words, Itô’s calculus cannot directly construct solutions unless the symbol is deterministic as discussed in Section
6
. Therefore, we initially focus on obtaining the existence of a solution with a deterministic symbol. Nonetheless, this assumption can be removed in the next section due to the linearity of the equations and the flexibility of the data.

We recall the conditions on the deterministic symbol
\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)
:

\displaystyle C^{\mathrm{e}|\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]|}_{R,T}:=\esssup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left|\exp\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\right|<\infty

(8.1)

and

\displaystyle C^{|\tilde{\psi}|}_{R,T}:=\esssup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\exp\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\mathrm{d}t\right)<\infty.

(8.2)

In particular, (
8.2
) implies that for almost every
\xi\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\tilde{\psi}(s,\xi)|\mathrm{d}s<\infty\quad\forall t\in(0,\infty).

(8.3)

Due to these assumptions on the symbol
\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)
,
all the stochastic integral terms can be rigorously handled through corollaries of the stochastic Fubini theorems developed in Section
6
.
Furthermore, recall that the deterministic term

\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s,

which appeared in (
6.1
).
We also need to control this deterministic term to find a solution
u
since we hope that our solution
u
satisfies (
6.1
).
Deterministic terms behave relatively well and can be easily treated by using the classical Fubini theorem.
Thus one can consider the weaker condition

\displaystyle C^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}_{R,T}:=\esssup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|<\infty

(8.4)

than (
8.1
).
Here is a prerequisite lemma for the deterministic terms to show the existence of a solution.
It also plays an important role in obtaining Theorem
3.25
.
A continuous extension to
\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
which is easily derived from integrals is also considered
as in Corollary
6.1
.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
U_{0}\in\mathbb{L}_{0}\left(\Omega\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and
F\in\mathbb{L}_{0}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{H}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)
.
Suppose that (
8.3
) holds and for any
R\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{T}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)|U_{0}(\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{T}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.87111pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.26662pt$]$}}(s)\left|F(s,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi<\infty\quad(a.s.)\quad\forall T\in(0,\infty).

(8.5)

Additionally, assume that for any
R\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)|\exp\left(\int_{0}^{\rho}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)|U_{0}(\xi)|\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)|\int_{0}^{\rho}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.87111pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.26662pt$]$}}(s)\left|F(s,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}\xi<\infty\quad(a.s.)\quad\forall T\in(0,\infty).

(8.6)

Denote

\displaystyle H(t,\xi)=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)U_{0}(\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.87111pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.26662pt$]$}}(s)\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)F(s,\xi)\mathrm{d}s.

Then
H
becomes an element in the intersection of the following two spaces

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{0,0,1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

In particular, (
8.5
) and (
8.6
) are satisfied if (
8.3
) and (
8.4
) hold with the conditions

\displaystyle U_{0}\in\mathbb{L}_{0,1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)\quad\text{and}\quad F\in\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{H}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right).

For this case, we additionally have

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{C}_{loc}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and the estimates that for all positive constants
T,R\in(0,\infty)

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|H(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\right)\left[\int_{B_{R}}|U_{0}(\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|F(s,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]~(a.s.)

(8.7)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||H(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\mathrm{d}t\right)\left[\int_{B_{R}}|U_{0}(\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|F(s,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]~(a.s.).

(8.8)

Proof.

Since there is no stochastic integral term in the definition of
H
, most parts of the proof are easy consequences of elementary measure theories. Therefore we skip the proof.
We only mention that (
8.3
) is not abundant since it is utilized to define
H
and show continuity of paths as discussed in Remark
3.26
and Remark
6.13
.
∎

Remark 8.2
.

A very similar definition of the space

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

was mentioned in Corollary
6.7
.
However, the definition of the space

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{0,0,1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

had never been mentioned.
Nonetheless, we omitted the precise definitions of these spaces in Lemma
8.1
since they can be easily inferred from previous definitions.

Remark 8.3
.

If conditions (
8.5
) and (
8.6
) in Lemma
8.1
are enhanced by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)|U_{0}(\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|F(s,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty\quad\forall T\in(0,\infty).

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)|\exp\left(\int_{0}^{\rho}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)|U_{0}(\xi)|\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)|\int_{0}^{\rho}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|F(s,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}\xi\right]<\infty\quad\forall T\in(0,\infty),

then
H
is in the intersection of the spaces

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

Additionally, if (
8.3
) and (
8.4
) are satisfied with the conditions

\displaystyle U_{0}\in\mathbb{L}_{1,1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)\quad\text{and}\quad F\in\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{H}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),

then

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right).

Finally, if
U_{0}
is
\mathscr{F}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable and
F
is
\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable, then
H
becomes
\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable.

Remark 8.4
.

If the data
U_{0}
and
F
in Lemma
8.1
are also non-random, then
(
8.7
) and (
8.8
) can be considered as deterministic estimates.
Consequently, this lemma is applicable even to a random symbol by utilizing these estimates for each
\omega
,
which is a crucial aspect in proving Corollary
9.1
in the subsequent section.

Since we solve equations on random time intervals
\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}
,
it needs to consider restrictions of classes consisting of functions on
\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
or
\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
.
These restrictions can be understood straightforwardly.
However, we provide more detail to demonstrate the rigor of our theories.
The following embedding properties are easily proved as in Proposition
2.33
since our
\tau
is a finite stopping time.

Lemma 8.5
.

(i)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

is a subspace of

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

(ii)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

is a subspace of

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

(iii)

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}_{loc}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

is a subspace of

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

(iv)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

is a subspace of

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

(v)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

is a subspace of

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

(vi)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

is a subspace of

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right).

Proof.

The statements (i)-(iii) might appear trivial given that
\tau
is a stopping time. The other statements (iv)-(vi) are derived from (i)-(iii) by simply taking expectations.
Nonetheless, we provide additional details to ensure clarity for the readers. Due to the similarity in the proofs, we only prove (ii) and (v).

Let

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

(8.9)

It suffices to show

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

(
8.9
) implies that there exists a
\Omega^{\prime}
such that
P(\Omega^{\prime})=1
and for any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}|H(t,\xi)||\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi<\infty\ \quad\forall R,T\in(0,\infty).

Since
\tau
is a finite stopping time, for each
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
, there exists a
T_{\omega}\in(0,\infty)
so that

\displaystyle\tau(\omega)\leq T_{\omega}.

Therefore, for all
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
and
R\in(0,\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}|H(t,\xi)||\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\leq\int_{0}^{T_{\omega}}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}|H(t,\xi)||\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi<\infty

(8.10)

and (ii) is proved.

To prove (v), consider a particular case of (
8.10
).
For any
\omega\in\Omega^{\prime}
and
R,T\in(0,\infty)
,
we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}|H(t,\xi)||\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}|H(t,\xi)||\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi<\infty.

(8.11)

Thus, if

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right),

then we have

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

by taking the expectations to both sides of (
8.11
).
The lemma is proved.
∎

Remark 8.6
.

Take the expectations to both sides of (
8.10
) and then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}|H(t,\xi)||\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T_{\omega}}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}|H(t,\xi)||\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

(8.12)

Generally, there is no guarantee that the right-hand side of (
8.12
) is finite even though

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

It is because the choice
T_{w}
is determined according to each sample point
\omega
.
Thus it turns out that the space

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times(0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

is not embedded into the space

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

unless the stopping time
\tau
is bounded.
Nevertheless, it is embedded into a slightly larger local space as mentioned in Lemma
8.5
.

Remark 8.7
.

Due to Theorem
2.4
, all results in Lemma
8.5
can be translated to their inverse transforms.
For instance,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{C}_{loc}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

is a subspace of

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right).

Theorem 8.8

(Existence of a Fourier-space weak solution with a deterministic symbol)
.

Let

\displaystyle u_{0}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),\quad f\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{H}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),

and

g\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}^{\omega,\xi,t}_{0,1,2,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d});l_{2}\right).

Suppose that
\tilde{\psi}
satisfies (
8.1
) and (
8.2
).
For each
(\omega,t)\in\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}
,
define

\displaystyle\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\mapsto\langle u(t,\cdot),\varphi\rangle

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\left(\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.87111pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.26662pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s\right)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.87111pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.26662pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}\right)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi

(8.13)

as a
\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-valued stochastic processes.
Then
u
becomes a Fourier-space weak solution to (
4.1
), which belongs to the intersection of the classes

\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

Proof.

Denote

U_{0}(\xi):=\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi),~F(t,\xi):=\mathcal{F}[f(t,\cdot)](\xi),~G^{k}(t,\xi):=\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(t,\cdot)](\xi),

\displaystyle H_{1}(t,\xi):=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)U_{0}(\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)F(s,\xi)\mathrm{d}s,

and

\displaystyle H_{2}(t,\xi):=\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)G^{k}(s,\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}.

First we show that
U_{0}
and
F
satisfy (
8.5
) and (
8.6
) in Lemma
8.1

by reminding all assumptions in the theorem.
However, it is very elementary that (
8.1
) and
the conditions

u_{0}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

f\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{H}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

imply (
8.5
). Thus we only show (
8.6
).
Reminding (
8.2
), the conditions on
u_{0}
and
f
, and the Fubini theorem, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)|\exp\left(\int_{0}^{\rho}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)|U_{0}(\xi)|\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)|\int_{0}^{\rho}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|F(s,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)|\exp\left(\int_{0}^{\rho}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathrm{d}\rho\right)\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{s}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathrm{d}\rho~1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)|\exp\left(\int_{0}^{\rho}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathrm{d}\rho\right)\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{\xi\in\mathbf{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq\rho}\left[\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathrm{d}\rho\right)\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{T}~1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi<\infty

with probability one for all
R,T\in(0,\infty)
.

Next, we show that (
8.3
) and (
8.4
) hold.
However, they are obviously valid as discussed at the beginning of the section.
Therefore by Lemmas
8.1
and
8.5
,

H_{1}
is in the intersection of two spaces

\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

Similarly, by Corollary
6.1
and Lemma
8.5
,

H_{2}
is in the intersection of the spaces

\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

Therefore, the sum

\displaystyle H:=H_{1}+H_{2}

is in the intersection

\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

Here we identify
H
with a
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-valued stochastic process (defined on
\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}
) on the basis of an
L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-inner product as follows: for each
(\omega,t)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}
,

\displaystyle\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\mapsto\langle H(t,\cdot),\varphi\rangle

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\left(\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right)\overline{\varphi(\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s\right)\overline{\varphi(\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\cdot)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)]\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}\right)\overline{\varphi(\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi.

Thus by the definitions of
u
in (
8.13
) and the Fourier transform on
\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
, for any
(\omega,t)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)],\varphi\rangle=\langle H(t,\cdot),\varphi\rangle,\quad\forall\varphi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d}).

In particular, for any
(\omega,t)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}
,

\xi\mapsto\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)
is a locally integrable function, which is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)=H(t,\xi)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}.

Additionally, since

\displaystyle H\in\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{F}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty)),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right),

we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(\rho,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}\rho

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)H(\rho,\xi)\mathrm{d}\rho

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)\exp\left(\int_{0}^{\rho}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)d\rho\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\rho}\left[\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\rho

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\rho}\left[\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}\mathrm{d}\rho\quad(a.e.)~(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}.

(8.14)
