Then by the fundamental theorem of calculus and the Fubini theorem,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\rho}\left[\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathrm{d}\rho\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\rho}\left[\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathrm{d}\rho\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B_{s}^{k}

(8.15)

for almost every
(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
, where two terms

\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s
and
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B_{s}^{k}
are legitimate due to the assumptions

f\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{H}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

g\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}^{\omega,\xi,t}_{0,1,2,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d});l_{2}\right).

By calculating the derivative of the compositions in (
8.15
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\left[\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathrm{d}\rho\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s}^{t}\left[\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathrm{d}\rho\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}

(8.16)

for almost every
(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
.
Additionally, applying the stochastic Fubini theorem and (
8.14
), one can easily check that the sum of terms in (
8.16
) is equal to

\displaystyle\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\rho}\left[\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\rho

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{\rho}\left[\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)\exp\left(\int_{s}^{\rho}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}\mathrm{d}\rho

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)-\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)\exp\left(\int_{0}^{\rho}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)d\rho\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(\rho,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}\rho+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(\rho,\xi)\mathcal{F}[u(\rho,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}\rho+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}

(8.17)

for almost every
(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
.
Finally, for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
, taking the integration with
\overline{\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}
in (
8.17
) and applying the definitions of actions on the elements of the class
\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
and the operator
\tilde{\psi}(s,-\mathrm{i}\nabla)
(Definition
2.7
) with the (stochastic) Fubini theorem, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle u(t,\cdot),\varphi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle u_{0},\varphi\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle\tilde{\psi}(s,-\mathrm{i}\nabla)u(s,\cdot),\varphi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle f(s,\cdot),\varphi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle g^{k}(s,\cdot),\varphi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}\quad(a.e.)~(\omega,t)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}.

Therefore
u
is a Fourier-space weak solution to (
4.1
)
by Definition
4.1
.
The theorem is proved.
∎

Remark 8.9
.

If
g=0
in Theorem
8.8
, then the condition (
8.1
) can be substituted with the less stringent condition (
8.4
). This is because the stronger assumption (
8.1
) is solely employed to establish the joint measurability of
H_{2}
in the proof of Theorem
8.8
.
Additionally, for this case
g=0
, the function
u
without the stochastic integral term becomes a solution to (
4.1
) even though the symbol is random.

Next, we show a stability result of the solution
u
obtained in the previous theorem.
Roughly saying, the spatial Fourier transform of the solution
u
is stable in terms of that of data.
The estimates for this stability result may depend on the constants

C^{\mathrm{e}|\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]|}_{R,T}
and
C^{|\tilde{\psi}|}_{R,T}

in (
8.1
) and (
8.2
).
In the following estimates, the smaller constant
C^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}_{R,T}
sometimes appears in stead of the constant
C^{\mathrm{e}|\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]|}_{R,T}
to optimize constants.
Additionally, the constants satisfy the inequalities

\displaystyle C^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}_{R,T}\leq C^{\mathrm{e}|\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]|}_{R,T}\leq C^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\tilde{\psi}]|}_{R,T}\leq 1+C^{|\tilde{\psi}|}_{R,T}

as explained in Remark
3.4
.
These inequalities help to simplify constants in the following Corollary.

Moreover, note that Theorem
7.3
does not contribute to optimizing constants in estimates, as kernel-based estimates are superior for the deterministic symbols.
However, if the symbol is random, then a representation by the kernel is not to be expected.
In this case, Theorem
7.3
can be used to optimize constants, as demonstrated in the proof of Corollary
9.5
. Nevertheless, Theorem
7.2
does not aid in improving estimates for the deterministic terms, even if the symbol is random, which will be revisited in Remark
9.2
.

Corollary 8.10
.

Let

\displaystyle u_{0}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,1,\ell oc}\left(\Omega\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),\quad f\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathscr{H}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right),

and

g\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}^{\omega,\xi,t}_{1,1,2,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d});l_{2}\right).

Suppose that
\tilde{\psi}
satisfies (
8.1
) and (
8.2
).
Then the solution
u
defined as (
8.13
) is in the intersection of the classes

\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

In particular,

u\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

u\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

if
\mathcal{F}[u_{0}]
is
\mathscr{F}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable and
\mathcal{F}[f]
is
\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable.
Moreover,
u
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,T}^{|\tilde{\psi}|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\cdot C_{R,T}^{|\tilde{\psi}|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right],

(8.18)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq T\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+T\cdot C_{BDG}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right],

(8.19)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}|\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right],

(8.20)

where
C_{R,T}^{|\tilde{\psi}|}
and
C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}
are given in (
8.2
) and (
8.4
).

Proof.

Due to Theorem
8.8
, it suffices to show (
8.18
), (
8.19
), and (
8.20
) since all the right hand sides of (
8.18
), (
8.19
), and (
8.20
) are finite.
We split
u
into two parts
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
.
Put

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u_{1}(t,\cdot)](\xi)=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u_{2}(t,\cdot)](\xi)=\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}.

Then it is obvious that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)=\mathcal{F}[u_{1}(t,\cdot)](\xi)+\mathcal{F}[u_{2}(t,\cdot)](\xi)\quad\forall(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}

and both
\mathcal{F}[u_{1}(t,\cdot)]
, and
\mathcal{F}[u_{2}(t,\cdot)]
can be defined on
\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
.
Firstly,
\mathcal{F}[u_{1}]
can be easily estimated (for the details, see the proof of Corollary
9.1
in the next section).
Indeed, by Lemma
8.1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[u_{1}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\mathcal{F}[u_{1}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u_{1}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u_{1}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\mathrm{d}t\right)

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

Next, we focus on estimating
\mathcal{F}[u_{2}]
.
It is also easily obtained from Corollary
6.10
with the Fubini theorem.
Indeed,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u_{2}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u_{2}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\sup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\mathrm{d}t\right)\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{2}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{2}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq T\cdot C_{BDG}\esssup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left[\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right],

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{2}(t,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{BDG}\left(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\exp\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

Combining all estimates above and recalling the definitions of the notations
C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}
,
C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}|\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}|]}
and
C_{R,T}^{|\tilde{\psi}|}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u_{1}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u_{2}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,T}^{|\tilde{\psi}|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi+C_{BDG}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}\left|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{1}(t,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{2}(t,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

The corollary is proved.
∎

Remark 8.11
.

It might seem odd that the constant
C^{\mathrm{e}|\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]|}_{R,T}
does not appear in (
8.19
) since the finiteness of
C^{\mathrm{e}|\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]|}_{R,T}
is a key assumption in the corollary.
However, this condition was utilized to show the joint measurability of
\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)
in order to apply the Fubini theorem as emphasized in Remarks
6.4
and
6.8
.
This difficulty of the joint measurability arose due to the stochastic integral terms.
Therefore for the case that
g=0
, it may be possible that the symbol is random and Assumption
3.1
is replaced with Assumption
3.21
,
which leads to the proof of Theorem
3.25
.
We postpone proving this special case until the next section where random symbols are addressed.

9.
Existence of a solution with a random symbol

In this section, we show that there exists a Fourier-space weak solution
u
to (
4.1
) even though the symbol
\psi

is random.
The function
u
in Theorem
8.8
which was a solution to (
4.1
) with a deterministic symbol cannot be defined in a legitimate way anymore.
This is because the stochastic integral term

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\psi(r,\cdot)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)]\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}

is not defined generally since the term
\int_{s}^{t}\psi(r,\cdot)\mathrm{d}r
related to the symbol is not
\mathscr{F}_{s}
-adapted and thus is not predictable as emphasized in Section
6
.
However, the other terms in (
8.13
) are still valid
and fortunately, the function
u
still becomes a solution to (
4.1
) in spite of the randomness on the symbol
\psi
if
g=0
. Therefore, we start showing the existence of our solution with this simple case.

Corollary 9.1
.

Suppose that all the assumptions in Theorem
3.25
hold and
define
u
as a
\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-valued stochastic process so that for each
(\omega,t)\in\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle\langle u(t,\cdot),\varphi\rangle

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\left(\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\psi(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\psi(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.87111pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.26662pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s\right)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi.

(9.1)

Then the function
u
becomes a Fourier-space weak solution to (
3.13
), which is in the intersection of the classes

\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\psi(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

Moreover, for all
R,T\in(0,\infty)
,
u
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}\left(|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\right)\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\psi]}\left(\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

(9.2)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\psi(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\leq C_{R,T}^{|\psi|}\left(\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

(9.3)

with probability one.

Proof.

Note that (
9.1
) is well-defined even though the symbol
\psi
is random since there are no stochastic integral terms.
Moreover, it is easy to show that the function
u
given by (
9.1
) is a Fourier space weak solution to (
3.13
) according to Theorem
8.8
(
cf
. Remark
8.9
).
Therefore, it suffices to show (
9.2
) and (
9.3
).

First, due to the definition of
u
, it is obvious that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\psi(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\psi(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\psi(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\psi(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s

for all
(\omega,t,\xi)\in\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
.
Observe that the domains of all functions above can be naturally extended to
\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d}
.
Put

\displaystyle H(t,\xi)=\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi),\quad U_{0}(\xi)=\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi),\quad\text{and}\quad F(t,\xi)=\mathcal{F}[f(t,\cdot)](\xi).

Then, applying Lemma
8.1
for each
\omega\in\Omega
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\right)\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq\int_{B_{R}}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|H(t,\xi)|\right)\mathrm{d}\xi

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\psi(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\right)\left[\int_{B_{R}}|U_{0}(\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|F(s,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle=C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\psi]}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\psi(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|\psi(t,\xi)||H(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\psi(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\psi(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\mathrm{d}t\right)\left[\int_{B_{R}}|U_{0}(\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|F(s,\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle=C_{R,T}^{|\psi|}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

(9.4)

Therefore, (
9.2
) and (
9.3
) are obtained.
The corollary is proved.
∎

Remark 9.2
.

Applying Theorem
7.2
with (
9.3
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\right)\mathrm{d}\xi\leq\left(1+C_{R,T}^{|\psi|}\right)\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

(9.5)

with probability one.
However, (
9.5
) does not give any better information than (
9.2
)
since

\displaystyle C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\psi]}\leq\left(1+C_{R,T}^{|\psi|}\right)

as discussed in Remark
3.4
and (
3.14
).

Remark 9.3
.

Recall that the logarithmic Laplacian operator
\log\Delta
whose symbol is given by
-\log|\xi|^{2}
satisfies Assumption
3.21
but it does not satisfy Assumption
3.22
.
Thus Theorem
3.25
is not applicable to (
3.13
) with logarithmic operators.
However, Assumption
3.22
in Theorem
3.25
also can be replaced with a weaker one to include logarithmic operators as mentioned in Remark
6.11
.
Specifically, the weaker condition consists of two parts as follows:

(i)

for almost every
\xi\in\mathbf{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}|\psi(s,\xi)|\mathrm{d}s<\infty\quad(a.s.)\quad\forall t\in(0,\infty).

(ii)

for all
R,T\in(0,\infty)
.

\displaystyle\esssup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\psi(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\psi(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\mathrm{d}t\right)<\infty\quad(a.s.).

Therefore, we can achieve the weak well-posedness of (
3.13
) with logarithmic operators, even though such results might be impossible for SPDEs.

Before going further to handle non-zero
g
, we consider a simple measurability issue for a predictable process.
Let
X_{t}
be a predictable process on
[0,\infty)
and assume that

\displaystyle\esssup_{\omega\in\Omega}|X_{t}|<\infty.

(9.6)

Then the mapping

t\in[0,\infty)\mapsto\esssup_{\omega\in\Omega}|X_{t}|

is
\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))
-measurable.
One can easily show it as least in two folds.
First, our probability space
\Omega
include Brownian motions and thus it could be derived from the Wiener space.
Thus we may assume that
\Omega
is separable.
On the other hand, any predictable process could be generated from an approximation of step processes and any step process possessing (
9.6
) satisfies the measurability.
Here
\mathscr{F}_{t}
-adapted process
X_{t}
is called a step process if

\displaystyle X_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}X_{k}1_{(t_{k-1},t_{k}]}(t),

for some
0\leq t_{0}\leq t_{1}\leq\cdots\leq t_{n}<\infty
.

Theorem 9.4

(Existence of a Fourier-space weak solution with a random symbol)
.

Suppose that all assumptions in Theorem
4.3
hold.
Then there exists a Fourier-space weak solution
u
to (
4.1
) in the intersection of the classes

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

(9.7)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\psi(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

(9.8)

Proof.

Denote

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi):=\esssup_{\omega\in\Omega}|\Re[\psi(t,\xi)]|+\mathrm{i}\esssup_{\omega\in\Omega}|\Im[\psi(t,\xi)]|.

Then
\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)
becomes a deterministic symbol satisfying (
8.1
) and (
8.2
) due to Assumptions
3.1
and
3.2
.
Thus by Theorem
8.8
, there exists a solution
\tilde{u}
in the intersection of the classes

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\psi(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

so that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\tilde{u}=\left(\tilde{\psi}(t,-\mathrm{i}\nabla)\tilde{u}(t,x)+f(t,x)\right)\mathrm{d}t+g^{k}(t,x)\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{t},\quad

\displaystyle(t,x)\in\Omega\times(0,\tau)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},

\displaystyle\tilde{u}(0,x)=u_{0},\quad

\displaystyle x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}

(9.9)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\tilde{u}(t,\cdot)](\xi)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\cdot)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)]\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}.

Put

\displaystyle\tilde{f}:=\psi(t,-\mathrm{i}\nabla)\tilde{u}-\tilde{\psi}(t,-\mathrm{i}\nabla)\tilde{u},

\displaystyle H_{1}(t,\xi)=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)+\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\mathrm{d}s,

and

\displaystyle H_{2}(t,\xi)=\int_{0}^{t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\tilde{\psi}(r,\cdot)\mathrm{d}r\right)1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(s)\mathcal{F}[g^{k}(s,\cdot)]\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}.

Then applying Assumptions
3.1
and
3.2
with Lemma
8.1
and Corollary
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle H_{1},H_{2}\in\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\Big(\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\Big).

Additionally, since
\psi(t,\xi)
is
\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable and

\displaystyle|\psi(t,\xi)|\leq|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\quad\forall(\omega,t,\xi)\in\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},

we have

\displaystyle\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\Big(\Omega\times[0,\infty)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\ \times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\Big).

In particular, by Lemma
8.5
,

\displaystyle\tilde{f}\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\Big(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\Big).

Thus, by Corollary
9.1
, there exists a solution
v

in the intersection of the classes

\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.49994pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{0,1,1,\ell oc}\Big(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}\left([0,\infty)\right)\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\Big)

so that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}v=\left(\psi(t,-\mathrm{i}\nabla)v(t,x)+\tilde{f}(t,x)\right)\mathrm{d}t,\quad

\displaystyle(t,x)\in\Omega\times(0,\tau)\times\mathbf{R}^{d},

\displaystyle v(0,x)=0,\quad

\displaystyle x\in\mathbf{R}^{d}.

(9.10)

Set

\displaystyle u(t,\xi)=\tilde{u}(t,\xi)+v(t,\xi).

Then, leveraging the linearity of equations (
9.9
) and (
9.10
), it is straightforward to demonstrate that
u
serves as a Fourier weak solution to (
4.1
).
Additionally,
u
belongs to the classes given in
(
9.7
) and (
9.8
) since both
\tilde{u}
and
v
are elements in these classes.
This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

Recall that our solution
u
in Theorem
9.4
cannot be represented by using kernels directly due to the randomness of the symbol. Consequently, obtaining optimal estimates from kernels, as shown in Corollary
8.10
, is not feasible. Finally, we believe that Theorem
7.3
may play a crucial role in achieving better estimates in the following corollary.

Corollary 9.5
.

Suppose that all the assumptions in Corollary
4.8
hold.
Then the solution
u
in Theorem
9.4
becomes an element in the intersection of the classes

\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\sigma\left(\mathscr{G}\times\mathcal{B}([0,\infty))\cup\mathscr{H}\cup\mathcal{P}\right)\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\psi(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right).

In particular,

u\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,loc}\mathbb{C}L_{1,\ell oc}\left(\text{$[$\kern-1.53325pt$[$}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right)

and

u\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{1,1,1,loc,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d}),|\psi(t,\xi)|\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}\xi\right)

if
\mathcal{F}[u_{0}]
is
\mathscr{F}_{0}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable and
\mathcal{F}[f]
is
\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-measurable.
Moreover,
u
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\psi(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{C}^{1}_{R,T,\psi}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\cdot\mathcal{C}^{1}_{R,T,\psi}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

(9.11)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{R,T,\psi}\wedge\mathcal{C}^{3}_{R,T,\psi}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\cdot\left(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{R,T,\psi}\wedge\mathcal{C}^{3}_{R,T,\psi}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

(9.12)

for all
R,T\in(0,\infty)
, where
\mathcal{C}^{i}_{R,T,\psi}

(i=1,2,3)
are positive constants which are explicitly given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{1}_{R,T,\psi}=\left[C_{R,T}^{\sup|\psi|}+2T\|\psi\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B_{R}\right)}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\right],

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{2}_{R,T,\psi}=C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\left[1+2T\|\psi\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B_{R}\right)}C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\right],

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{3}_{R,T,\psi}=1+\mathcal{C}^{1}_{R,T,\psi}.

Proof.

Let
R
and
T
be positive constants.
By Theorem
9.4
, there exists a Fourier-space weak solution
u
to (
4.1
) in a larger class.
As shown in the proof of Theorem
9.4
, this solution
u
can be split into two parts

u=\tilde{u}+v
, where
\tilde{u}
satisfies (
9.9
) and
v
satisfies (
9.10
).
Since our data
u_{0}
and
f
are enhanced with finite expectations, by Corollary
8.10
, the solution
\tilde{u}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[\tilde{u}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,T}^{|\tilde{\psi}|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\cdot C_{R,T}^{|\tilde{\psi}|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

(9.13)

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[\tilde{u}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}|\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

(9.14)

On the other hand, by Corollary
9.1
,
v
satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[v(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\right)\mathrm{d}\xi\leq C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\psi]}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[\tilde{f}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\quad(a.s.)

(9.15)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{B_{R}}|\psi(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[v(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\leq C_{R,T}^{|\psi|}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[\tilde{f}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\quad(a.s.),

(9.16)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{f}:=\psi(t,-\mathrm{i}\nabla)\tilde{u}-\tilde{\psi}(t,-\mathrm{i}\nabla)\tilde{u}.

(9.17)

Observe that

\displaystyle C^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]}_{R,T}\leq C^{\mathrm{e}|\int\Re[\tilde{\psi}]|}_{R,T}

\displaystyle=\esssup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left[\exp\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\esssup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left[\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\esssup_{\omega\in\Omega}\left|\Re[\psi(r,\xi)]\right|\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]=C^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}_{R,T},

\displaystyle C^{|\tilde{\psi}|}_{R,T}

\displaystyle=\esssup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}|\tilde{\psi}(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\left|\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\tilde{\psi}(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right|\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle=\esssup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\esssup_{\omega\in\Omega}|\psi(t,\xi)|\left[\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\esssup_{\omega\in\Omega}\left|\Re[\psi(r,\xi)]\right|\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathrm{d}t\right]=C^{\sup|\psi|}_{R,T},

\displaystyle C^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\psi]}_{R,T}

\displaystyle=\esssup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T,\xi\in B_{R}}\left[\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\psi(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\esssup_{(t,\xi)\in(0,T)\times B_{R}}\left[\exp\left(\int_{0}^{t}\esssup_{\omega\in\Omega}\left|\Re[\psi(r,\xi)]\right|\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]=C^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}_{R,T}\quad(a.s.),

and

\displaystyle C^{|\psi|}_{R,T}

\displaystyle=\esssup_{\xi\in B_{R}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}|\psi(t,\xi)|\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\exp\left(\left|\int_{s}^{t}\Re[\psi(r,\xi)]\mathrm{d}r\right|\right)\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\esssup_{(\omega,\xi)\in\Omega\times B_{R}}\left[\int_{0}^{T}|\psi(t,\xi)|\left[\esssup_{\omega\in\Omega,0\leq s\leq t}\exp\left(\int_{s}^{t}\left|\Re[\psi(r,\xi)]\right|\mathrm{d}r\right)\right]\mathrm{d}t\right]=C^{\sup|\psi|}_{R,T}\quad(a.s.).

Thus by Fubini’s theorem, (
9.17
), and (
9.14
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[\tilde{f}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\|\psi\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B_{R}\right)}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[\tilde{u}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2T\|\psi\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B_{R}\right)}C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]\Bigg).

(9.18)

Since
u=\tilde{u}+v
, applying (
9.13
) and (
9.16
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\psi(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\psi(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[\tilde{u}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\psi(t,\xi)||\mathcal{F}[v(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,T}^{\sup|\psi|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\sup|\psi|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{R,T}^{\sup|\psi|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[\tilde{f}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

(9.19)

Additionally, due to (
9.18
), the sum of the last three terms in (
9.19
) is less than or equal to

\displaystyle\left[C_{R,T}^{\sup|\psi|}+2T\|\psi\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B_{R}\right)}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\left[C_{R,T}^{\sup|\psi|}+2T\|\psi\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B_{R}\right)}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

(9.20)

Therefore (
9.19
) and (
9.20
) lead to (
9.11
).
Next, we show (
9.12
) similarly.
We apply (
9.14
) and (
9.15
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[\tilde{u}(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[v(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[\tilde{f}(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi.

Applying (
9.18
) to the last terms in the above inequality, we additionally obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\left[1+2T\|\psi\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B_{R}\right)}C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\left[1+2T\|\psi\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B_{R}\right)}C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\times\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

(9.21)

On the other hand, we apply Theorem
7.3
with (
9.11
).
Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau\wedge T]}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\left[C_{R,T}^{\sup|\psi|}+2T\|\psi\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B_{R}\right)}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{BDG}\left(1+\left[C_{R,T}^{\sup|\psi|}+2T\|\psi\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B_{R}\right)}\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\times\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right].

(9.22)

Finally, (
9.21
) and (
9.22
) complete (
9.12
).
The corollary is proved.
∎

Remark 9.6
.

The first
\mathcal{C}^{2}_{R,T,\psi}
in (
9.12
) can be slightly weaken by

\displaystyle\esssup_{\omega\in\Omega}\left(C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\Re[\psi]}\right)+2T\cdot C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}\|\psi\|_{L_{\infty}\left(\Omega\times(0,T)\times B_{R}\right)}C_{R,T}^{\mathrm{e}\int\sup|\Re[\psi]|}.

(9.23)

Moreover, (
9.12
) leads to the inequality

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq T\left(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{R,T,\psi}\wedge\mathcal{C}^{3}_{R,T,\psi}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u_{0}](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi+\int_{B_{R}}\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[f(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|\mathrm{d}s\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\quad+T\cdot C_{BDG}\cdot\left(\mathcal{C}^{2}_{R,T,\psi}\wedge\mathcal{C}^{3}_{R,T,\psi}\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\left|\mathcal{F}[g(s,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right],

for all
R,T\in(0,\infty)
.
Here even the second
\mathcal{C}^{2}_{R,T,\psi}
can be replaced with the constant from (
9.23
) by directly estimating

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\int_{B_{R}}|\mathcal{F}[u(t,\cdot)](\xi)|\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

based on (
8.19
) in Corollary
8.10
instead of relying on (
9.12
).

10.
Proofs of Theorem
4.3
, Corollary
4.8
, and Theorem
3.25

We now proceed to prove Theorem
4.3
, Corollary
4.8
, and Theorem
3.25
. The necessary background for these proofs had been established in previous sections. Our task is to synthesize this information to reach the stated conclusions.

Specifically, we established the uniqueness and existence of a Fourier-space weak solution in Section
7
and Section
9
, respectively. Therefore, we need only verify that our solution classes align with both uniqueness and existence criteria. Fortunately, we had already demonstrated that our solution is unique within a large class, as shown in Theorem
7.6
, which encompasses all classes considered for the existence of a solution.

We conclude this section by succinctly connecting the prerequisites to reach our final conclusions.

Proof of Theorem
4.3

Recall that we found a Fourier-space weak solution
u
in Theorem
9.4
under all assumptions in Theorem
4.3
.
We also proved the uniqueness of a Fourier-space weak solution in a larger class than the one containing
u
in Theorem
7.6
. Therefore, the theorem is proved.
∎

Proof Corollary
4.8

Recall that Assumption
3.15
obviously implies both Assumptions
3.1
and
3.2
.
Therefore, based on Theorem
4.3
, it is sufficient to demonstrate that the solution
u
satisfies (
4.6
) and (
4.7
). This had already been established in Corollary
9.5
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.25

Given the uniqueness of the solution in Theorem
7.6
, it is sufficient to show that the solution

u
in Corollary
9.1
satisfies (
3.15
) and (
3.16
).
However, it is also obtained in the same corollary.

11.
proof of Theorem
3.11

Recall that a space-time white noise can be constructed from a sequence of independent one-dimensional Brownian motions
B_{t}^{k}
according to (
2.3
) with the help of an orthogonal basis
\eta_{k}
on
L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
.
Also, remember that we additionally assumed that all elements in the orthogonal basis are in the Schwartz class
\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
.
Due to this additional assumption and Parseval’s identity, we can explicitly calculate the
l_{2}
-values of the sequence formed by the Fourier transform of the product
\eta_{k}
and an element
h
in
L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
in terms of the
L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-norm of
h
. Here are the details.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
\{\eta_{k}:k\in\mathbf{N}\}
be an
L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-orthonomal basis such that
\eta_{k}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
for all
k\in\mathbf{N}
.
Then for any
h\in L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{F}[\eta h](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}=(2\pi d)^{d}\|h\|^{2}_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\quad(a.e.)~\xi\in\mathbf{R}^{d},

where

\mathcal{F}[\eta h]=\left(\mathcal{F}[\eta_{1}h],\mathcal{F}[\eta_{2}h],\ldots\right).

Proof.

For each
k\in\mathbf{N}
,
\eta_{k}h\in L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
.
Thus the Fourier (Plancherel) transform of
\eta_{k}h
is well-defined as an element in
L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
.
Additionally, by some properties of the Fourier and inverse Fourier transforms,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\eta_{k}h](\xi)=(2\pi d)^{d/2}\mathcal{F}[\eta_{k}]\ast\mathcal{F}[h](\xi):=(2\pi d)^{d/2}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[\eta_{k}](\zeta)\mathcal{F}[h](\xi-\zeta)\mathrm{d}\zeta.

Observe that
\{\mathcal{F}[\eta_{k}]:k\in\mathbf{N}\}
is another orthonomal basis on
L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
due to the Plancherel theorem.
Thus for each
\xi
, applying Parseval’s identity and the Plancherel theorem, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{F}[\eta h](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}=(2\pi d)^{d}\|\mathcal{F}[h](\xi-\cdot)\|^{2}_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}=(2\pi d)^{d}\|h\|^{2}_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}.

The lemma is proved.
∎

This identity allows us to regard our main equation (
2.2
) as a specific case of (
4.1
).
In particular, the stochastic differential term
h(t,x)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{B}_{t}
in (
2.2
) becomes well-defined if

h\in\mathbb{L}_{0,2,2}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right).

We show this in the following corollary.

Corollary 11.2
.

Let

h\in\mathbb{L}_{0,2,2}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.4755pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.53325pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})\right).

Then for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
, the stochastic term

\int_{0}^{t}\left\langle h(s,\cdot)\eta^{k}(\cdot),\varphi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}:=\int_{0}^{t}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.87111pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.26662pt$]$}}(s)\left\langle h(s,\cdot)\eta^{k}(\cdot),\varphi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}B^{k}_{s}

in (
3.9
) is well-defined.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
.
It suffices to show that for any
T\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(t)\left|\left\langle h(t,\cdot)\eta^{k}(\cdot),\varphi\right\rangle\right|_{l_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t<\infty\quad(a.s.).

For each
t\in(0,\infty)
, applying Lemma
11.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{F}[\eta h(t,\cdot)](\xi)\right|^{2}_{l_{2}}=(2\pi d)^{d}\|h(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\quad(a.e.)~\xi\in\mathbf{R}^{d},

(11.1)

In particular,
\left|\mathcal{F}[\eta h(t,\cdot)](\xi)\right|_{l_{2}}
is locally integrable on
\mathbf{R}^{d}
.
Thus we have

\displaystyle\eta(\cdot)h(t,\cdot)\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbf{R}^{d};l_{2}).

and for each
\varphi\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
and
k\in\mathbf{N}
,

\displaystyle\left\langle h(t,\cdot)\eta^{k}(\cdot),\varphi\right\rangle=\int_{\mathbf{R}^{d}}\mathcal{F}[h(t,\cdot)\eta^{k}](\xi)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi=\int_{\supp\mathcal{F}[\varphi]}\mathcal{F}[h(t,\cdot)\eta^{k}](\xi)\overline{\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)}\mathrm{d}\xi.

(11.2)

Therefore, combining (
11.1
) and (
11.2
) with the generalized Minkowski inequality, for each
T\in(0,\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}1_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}}(t)\left|\left\langle h(t,\cdot)\eta^{k}(\cdot),\varphi\right\rangle\right|_{l_{2}}^{2}\mathrm{d}t\leq(2\pi d)^{d}\left|\supp\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right|\sup_{\xi\in\mathbf{R}^{d}}|\mathcal{F}[\varphi](\xi)|\int_{0}^{\tau}\|h(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\mathrm{d}t<\infty\quad(a.s.),

where
\left|\supp\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\right|
denotes the Lebesgue measure of
\supp\mathcal{F}[\varphi]
and is finite since
\mathcal{F}[\varphi]\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
.
The corollary is proved.
∎

Now, Theorem
3.11
follows as a corollary of Theorem
4.3
.

Proof of Theorem
3.11

Recall that
\{\eta_{k}:k\in\mathbf{N}\}
is an
L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
-orthonomal basis so that
\eta_{k}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbf{R}^{d})
for all
k\in\mathbf{N}
.
Put

g=\left(g^{1},g^{2},\ldots\right)=\left(\eta_{1}h,\eta_{2}h,\ldots\right).

Then for each
R\in(0,\infty)
, applying Hölder’s inequality, Fubini’s theorem, and Lemma
11.1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}|\mathcal{F}[g(t,\cdot)](\xi)|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\leq R\left(\int_{0}^{\tau}\|h(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}<\infty.

Thus

g\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}^{\omega,\xi,t}_{0,1,2,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d});l_{2}\right).

Therefore, Theorem
3.11
is obtained from Theorem
4.3
.

Proof of Corollary
3.17

Put

g=\left(g^{1},g^{2},\ldots\right)=\left(\eta_{1}h,\eta_{2}h,\ldots\right)

as in the proof of Theorem
3.11
.
Then for each
R,T\in(0,\infty)
, applying Hölder’s inequality, Fubini’s theorem, and Lemma
11.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B_{R}}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}|\mathcal{F}[g(t,\cdot)](\xi)|^{2}_{l_{2}}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\mathrm{d}\xi\right]

\displaystyle\leq R\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\|h(t,\cdot)\|^{2}_{L_{2}(\mathbf{R}^{d})}\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\right]<\infty.

(11.3)

Thus

g\in\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathbb{L}^{\omega,\xi,t}_{1,1,2,\ell oc}\left(\text{\,\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-3.39996pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.49994pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d},\mathcal{P}\times\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{R}^{d})|_{\text{\,\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{${\scriptstyle|}$}\kern-2.71053pt$($}0,\tau\text{$]$\kern-1.19579pt$]$}\times\mathbf{R}^{d}};l_{2}\right).

Therefore, Corollary
3.17
is obtained from Corollary
4.8
with (
11.3
).
